    # So our condition that an edge is "important" if it's in any shortest path is correct: if it's not in any shortest path, 
    # then even if we remove it, we still have shortest paths for both constraints.
    
    # But what about edges that are in some longer path but not in any shortest path? Can we remove them?
    
    # Yes! Because the constraint is about getting there in at most l hours, not exactly shortest path length.
    
    # However, if we remove an edge that is in a longer path, but there's another path of the required length, we can still satisfy the constraint.
    
    # But our shortest path already guarantees there's a path of length ≤ l. If we remove an edge that's not in any shortest path, 
    # does that break the existence of a path of length ≤ l?
    
    # No, because the shortest path is still there (since it doesn't use that edge).
    
    # Actually, if an edge is not part of any shortest path, then there exists a shortest path that doesn't use it, so removing it doesn't affect the shortest path constraint.
    
    # Therefore, we can safely remove any edge that is not part of any shortest path from s1 to t1 or from s2 to t2.
    
    # But wait: what if removing an edge makes the shortest path longer than l? 
    # For example, if there are multiple shortest paths, removing one doesn't make the shortest path longer.
    # The shortest path is defined as the minimum possible distance. 
    # If there are multiple shortest paths, removing one doesn't increase the shortest distance.
    
    # Therefore, if an edge is not used in any shortest path, removing it won't increase the shortest path length.
    
    # So we can remove all edges that are not part of any shortest path.
    
    # Algorithm:
    # 1. Check if we can reach s1 to t1 in ≤ l1 hours and s2 to t2 in ≤ l2 hours using shortest paths
    # 2. If not, return -1
    # 3. Find all edges that are part of some shortest path from s1 to t1 or from s2 to t2
    # 4. Answer = m - (count of such edges)
    
    # How to find edges that are part of some shortest path?
    
    # For each node, we can find shortest distances to s1 and to t1, and to s2 and to t2.
    
    # For a path from s1 to t1 to be of length d, we need d ≤ l1.
    # An edge (u,v) is part of some shortest path from s1 to t1 if:
    #   dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_v[t1] == dist_s1[t1]  AND  dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_v[t1] == d
    # Actually: dist_s1[u] + dist_v[t1] + 1 == dist_s1[t1]
    
    # Similarly for s2 to t2.
    
    # Implementation:
    
    # Step 1: find shortest distances
    dist_s1_to_all = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s1_to_all[s1] = 0
    queue = deque([s1])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s1_to_all[v] == -1:
                dist_s1_to_all[v] = dist_s1_to_all[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    dist_s1_to_t1 = dist_s1_to_all[t1]
    if dist_s1_to_t1 > l1:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    dist_s2_to_all = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s2_to_all[s2] = 0
    queue = deque([s2])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s2_to_all[v] == -1:
                dist_s2_to_all[v] = dist_s2_to_all[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    dist_s2_to_t2 = dist_s2_to_all[t2]
    if dist_s2_to_t2 > l2:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Step 2: find edges that are part of shortest path from s1 to t1
    shortest_path_edges_s1_t1 = set()
    for u in range(1, n + 1):
        if dist_s1_to_all[u] != -1 and u != s1 and u != t1:
            for v in graph[u]:
                if v != u and dist_s1_to_all[v] != -1:
                    # Check if edge (u,v) is in some shortest path
                    # This edge can be part of a shortest path if:
                    # dist_s1_to_all[u] + 1 + dist_s1_to_all[v] == dist_s1_to_t1
                    if dist_s1_to_all[u] + 1 + dist_s1_to_all[v] == dist_s1_to_t1:
                        # We need to ensure we don't double count
                        # Add both directions
                        shortest_path_edges_s1_t1.add((min(u,v), max(u,v)))
    
    # Step 3: find edges that are part of shortest path from s2 to t2
    shortest_path_edges_s2_t2 = set()
    for u in range(1, n + 1):
        if dist_s2_to_all[u] != -1 and u != s2 and u != t2:
            for v in graph[u]:
                if v != u and dist_s2_to_all[v] != -1:
                    if dist_s2_to_all[u] + 1 + dist_s2_to_all[v] == dist_s2_to_t2:
                        shortest_path_edges_s2_t2.add((min(u,v), max(u,v)))
    
    # Step 4: combine both sets
    total_shortest_path_edges = shortest_path_edges_s1_t1 | shortest_path_edges_s2_t2
    
    # Step 5: count how many edges in the original list are in this set
    edges_to_keep = 0
    for a, b in edges:
        edge_key = (min(a,b), max(a,b))
        if edge_key in total_shortest_path_edges:
            edges_to_keep += 1
    
    # Answer is m - edges_to_keep
    print(m - edges_to_keep)

solve()

Wait, I need to be careful: the condition `dist_s1_to_all[u] + 1 + dist_s1_to_all[v] == dist_s1_to_t1` ensures that the path u-v is part of a shortest path from s1 to t1.

But what if there are multiple shortest paths? This condition will find all edges that are part of at least one shortest path.

Yes, this logic is correct.

Let me verify with examples:

Example 1: 
- s1=1, t1=3, l1=2
- s2=3, t2=5, l2=2
- Shortest path 1->3: 1->2->3 (length 2)
- Shortest path 3->5: 3->4->5 (length 2)

For edge (1,2): dist[1]=0, dist[2]=1, 0+1+dist[2]=0+1+1=2 == dist[1->3]=2 -> included
For edge (2,3): dist[2]=1, dist[3]=2, 1+1+2=4 ≠ 2 -> not included? 

Wait, no: for edge (2,3): u=2, v=3, dist[2]=1, dist[3]=2, so 1+1+2=4 ≠ 2. 
But this edge should be in the path 1->2->3.

I made a mistake in the condition.

For edge (2,3): when u=2, v=3: dist[u] + 1 + dist[v] = 1 + 1 + 2 = 4 ≠ 2.

But we need: dist[u] + 1 + dist[v] should equal the total distance.

In the path 1->2->3: we have dist[1]=0, dist[2]=1, dist[3]=2.
For edge (1,2): dist[1] + 1 + dist[2] = 0 + 1 + 1 = 2 ✓
For edge (2,3): dist[2] + 1 + dist[3] = 1 + 1 + 2 = 4 ≠ 2 ✗

This is wrong.

The issue is: for an edge (u,v), if we're going from s1 to t1 via u-v, then the total distance is dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v]. 
But this only equals dist_s1[t1] if u is before v in the path, and v is after.

Actually, in the path s1 -> ... -> u -> v -> ... -> t1, the distance is dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v].

For edge (2,3): we have s1=1, u=2, v=3, dist_s1[2]=1, dist_s1[3]=2, so 1 + 1 + 2 = 4 ≠ 2.

This can't be right.

I see the error: the condition should be that dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v] == dist_s1[t1] should hold for the edge (u,v) when we go from s1 to u to v to t1.

But the distance from u to v is 1, so total distance is dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v].

For (2,3): dist[2]=1, dist[3]=2, so 1 + 1 + 2 = 4, which is not 2.

This suggests my distance calculation is wrong.

Let me recalculate: in a chain 1-2-3-4-5, distances from s1=1 to each node:
- dist[1] = 0
- dist[2] = 1
- dist[3] = 2
- dist[4] = 3
- dist[5] = 4

Path from 1 to 3: length 2.

For edge (2,3): if we go 1->2->3, the distance is 2. 
dist[2] + 1 + dist[3] = 1 + 1 + 2 = 4 ≠ 2.

The problem is: we're using dist_s1[v] which is the distance from s1 to v, but in the path s1->u->v->t1, the distance from v to t1 should be dist_s1[t1] - (dist_s1[u] + 1) only if v is after u.

Actually, the condition should be: dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v] == dist_s1[t1] only if u is before v in the path.

But in our case, from 1 to 3, u=2, v=3, we want u to be closer to s1 than v.

The correct condition is: dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_s1[v] == dist_s1[t1] and u is on the path to v.

But for edge (2,3): dist_s1[2] + 1 + dist_s1[3] = 1 + 1 + 2 = 4 ≠ 2.

Clearly wrong.

I see the error: dist_s1[v] is the distance from s1 to v, which is the shortest distance. In the path, from v to t1, the distance is dist_s1[t1] - dist_s1[v] only if v is on the path from s1 to t1.

For any edge (u,v) to be part of a shortest path from s1 to t1, we need:
dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_t1[v] == dist_s1[t1]  OR  dist_s1[v] + 1 + dist_t1[u] == dist_s1[t1]

That is, either going from u to v, or from v to u, in a shortest path.

Yes! That's the correct condition.

So for an edge (u,v), it is in some shortest path from s1 to t1 if:
dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_t1[v] == dist_s1[t1]  OR  dist_s1[v] + 1 + dist_t1[u] == dist_s1[t1]

Similarly for s2 to t2.

Let me verify with (2,3) in example 1:
- s1=1, t1=3, dist_s1[2]=1, dist_t1[3]=0, dist_s1[t1]=2
- dist_s1[2] + 1 + dist_t1[3] = 1 + 1 + 0 = 2 == dist_s1[t1] ✓
- So this edge is in a shortest path.

For edge (3,4):
- s1=1, t1=3, dist_s1[3]=2, dist_t1[4]=1 (since from 3 to 4, then to 5, from 3 to 4: dist_3[4]=1)
- dist_s1[3] + 1 + dist_t1[4] = 2 + 1 + 1 = 4 ≠ 2
- dist_s1[4] + 1 + dist_t1[3] = 3 + 1 + 0 = 4 ≠ 2
- So not in a shortest path? 

But in the path 3->4->5, from s2=3 to t2=5, it should be in shortest path.

For s2 to t2: s2=3, t2=5
- dist_s2[3]=0, dist_t2[5]=2 (distance from 5 to 5 is 0? dist_t2[5]=0)
- For edge (3,4): dist_s2[3] + 1 + dist_t2[4] = 0 + 1 + 1 = 2 == dist_s2[5]=2 ✓

Yes! So in the s2 to t2 constraint, it is included.

So the algorithm is:

1. Compute shortest distances from s1 to all nodes (dist_s1)
2. Compute shortest distances from t1 to all nodes (dist_t1)
3. For each edge (u,v), check if dist_s1[u] + 1 + dist_t1[v] == dist_s1[t1] OR dist_s1[v] + 1 + dist_t1[u] == dist_s1[t1]
4. Similarly for s2 to t2
5. Combine all such edges
6. Count how many of the original edges are in this set
7. Answer = m - count

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    edges = []
    graph = [[] for _ in range(n+1)]
    
    for _ in range(m):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
    
    s1, t1, l1 = map(int, input().split())
    s2, t2, l2 = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Compute shortest distances from s1 to all nodes
    dist_s1 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s1[s1] = 0
    queue = deque([s1])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s1[v] == -1:
                dist_s1[v] = dist_s1[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from t1 to all nodes
    dist_t1 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_t1[t1] = 0
    queue = deque([t1])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_t1[v] == -1:
                dist_t1[v] = dist_t1[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from s2 to all nodes
    dist_s2 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s2[s2] = 0
    queue = deque([s2])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s2[v] == -1:
                dist_s2[v] = dist_s2[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from t2 to all nodes
    dist_t2 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_t2[t2] = 0
    queue = deque([t2])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_t2[v] == -1:
                dist_t2[v] = dist_t2[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Check if constraints are satisfied
    if dist_s1[t1] > l1 or dist_s2[t2] > l2:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Find edges that are part of any shortest path from s1 to t1
    critical_edges = set()
    
    # For s1 to t1
    for a, b in edges:
        # Check if edge (a,b) is in shortest path from s1 to t1
        if (dist_s1[a] + 1 + dist_t1[b] == dist_s1[t1] or 
            dist_s1[b] + 1 + dist_t1[a] == dist_s1[t1]):
            critical_edges.add((min(a,b), max(a,b)))
    
    # For s2 to t2
    for a, b in edges:
        if (dist_s2[a] + 1 + dist_t2[b] == dist_s2[t2] or 
            dist_s2[b] + 1 + dist_t2[a] == dist_s2[t2]):
            critical_edges.add((min(a,b), max(a,b)))
    
    # Count how many original edges are critical
    count_critical = 0
    for a, b in edges:
        if (min(a,b), max(a,b)) in critical_edges:
            count_critical += 1
    
    print(m - count_critical)

solve()
```

from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    edges = []
    graph = [[] for _ in range(n+1)]
    
    for _ in range(m):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a, b))
        graph[a].append(b)
        graph[b].append(a)
    
    s1, t1, l1 = map(int, input().split())
    s2, t2, l2 = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Compute shortest distances from s1 to all nodes
    dist_s1 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s1[s1] = 0
    queue = deque([s1])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s1[v] == -1:
                dist_s1[v] = dist_s1[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from t1 to all nodes
    dist_t1 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_t1[t1] = 0
    queue = deque([t1])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_t1[v] == -1:
                dist_t1[v] = dist_t1[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from s2 to all nodes
    dist_s2 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_s2[s2] = 0
    queue = deque([s2])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_s2[v] == -1:
                dist_s2[v] = dist_s2[u] + 1
                queue.append(v)
    
    # Compute shortest distances from t2 to all nodes
    dist_t2 = [-1] * (n + 1)
    dist_t2[t2] = 0
    queue = deque([t2])
    while queue:
        u = queue.popleft()
        for v in graph[u]:
            if dist_t2[v] == -1:
                dist_t2[v] = dist_t2[u] + 1

            critical_edges.add((min(a,b), max(a,b)))
    
    # Count how many original edges are critical
    count_critical = 0
    for a, b in edges:
        if (min(a,b), max(a,b)) in critical_edges:
            count_critical += 1
    
    print(m - count_critical)
